# Database Management Systems — syllabus

94 syllabus section(s) for this course in the archive, newest batch first. Text comes from the PDF (OCR for scanned pages; check figures against the source). All programmes' syllabi: [`syllabi/`](../../syllabi/README.md).

## MCA · All branches · batch 2026-2028 · Sem 1

- Course as printed: Database Management Systems (code 703AI0C003)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/MCA/Batch ( 2026 To 2028 )/Semester - 1.pdf`, page 3-5

````text
Program: Master of Computer Applications (MCA)   Semester: I
Course: Database Management Systems   Code: 703AI0C003
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours per   (Hours per   (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Nil
Course Objective
The objective of the course is to provide a comprehensive introduction to the fundamental concepts for
design and development of database systems, with an emphasis on how to organize, maintain and retrieve
- efficiently, and effectively - information from a database management system
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Describe core concepts of database and model a database management system through ER
modelling
2. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structured query language to retrieve and manage
data from relational database
3. Demonstrate the use of normalization for database design
4. Demonstrate the concept of transactions and use modern database techniques such as NoSQL
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
Introduction
1.   Database System Applications, Purpose of Database Systems, View of Data,   03
Database Languages, Data Models, Database Users and Administrator
Database Design and the E-R Model
Overview of the Design Process, The Entity-Relationship Model, Constraints, Entity   05
2.
Relationship Diagrams, Reduction to Relational Schemas, Schema Diagrams , Entity-
Relationship Design Issues, Extended ER features
Introduction to the Relational Model
3.   Structure of Relational Databases, Database Schema, Keys, Relational Algebra, Basic   03
operators of Relational Algebra,
Structured Query Language
Overview of the SQL Query Language, SQL Data Definition, SQL Constraints, Basic   06
4.
Structure of SQL Queries, Additional Basic Operations, DML operations, Set
operations, Aggregate Functions, Nested Sub-queries, Joins, views
Signature
(Head of the Department)
A.Y 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Relational Database Design
Features of Good Relational Designs, Problems with bad design, Decomposition
5.   using concept of functional dependencies, Armstrong’s axioms, Closure of   05
functional dependency, Closure of attribute, Introduction to process of
Normalization and de-normalization, Normal Forms- 1NF, 2NF, 3NF, BCNF
Transactions
6.   What is Transactions? Properties of transaction, Transaction states, Issues with   04
concurrent executions, Schedules, Serializability- Conflict and View
Introduction to NoSQL
Overview of NoSQL, characteristics of NoSQL, Storage types of NoSQL,
7.   Implementing NoSQL in MongoDB - Managing Databases and Collections from   04
the MongoDB shell, Finding Documents in MongoDB collection from the
MongoDB shell.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Hennery Korth and Abraham Silberschatz, Database System Concepts, 7th Edition, McGraw Hill, 2019.
2. Elmarsi and Navathe, Fundamentals of Database Design, 7th Edition, Addison Wesley, 2019.
3. A Phaltankar, J. Ahsan, M. Harrison, L. Nevdov, MondoDB Fundamental, Packt Publishing, 2020.
Reference Books
1. Bob Bryla, Kevin Loney Oracle Database 12C The Complete Reference, 1st Edition, Tata McGraw Hill,
2017.
2. Marko Aleksendric, Arek Borucki, Mastering MongoDB 7.0, Packt Publishing, 2024.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
Signature
(Head of the Department)
A.Y 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: Master of Computer Applications (MCA)   Semester: I
````

## B TECH · Data Science · AY 2025-26 · Sem 4

- Course as printed: Database Management System (code 702CO0C067)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH/2ND YEAR/COMPUTER ENGINEERING (CSDS) 311/SEM IV/B Tech CSEDS 311 (VT) Sem IV Syllabus AY 2025-26 (1).pdf`, page 7-10

````text
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Engineering
Semester: IV
(Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course: Database Management System   Code: 702CO0C067
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per
week)   week)   per week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving, Data structures
Course Objective
To develop an understanding of essential database concepts. To understand different issues
involved in the design and implementation of a database systems. To understand and use database
query languages to access and manage a database. To design and build a simple database system
with modeling, designing and implementation To understand the need of data analytics and
advanced database systems like data warehouse and data mining
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Describe the terminology, features and basic concepts of database systems
2. Formulate queries using structured query language and relational algebra
3. Design a model expressed in the form of an entity relationship diagram and transform it into a
relational model
4. Demonstrate an understanding of normalization theory, transactions and need for data analytics
and advanced database models
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Database Management Systems   3
Role and advantages of the DBMS, Types of Databases, Importance of database
design, Evolution of file system, Problems with file system, Database System
environment, DBMS functions, Data models, importance of data models, types
of data models, Data abstraction (Book   : “Database System Design,
Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
2   Introduction to Relational Model   4
Structure of relational databases- relation, attribute, tuple, domain, Codd’s
rules, Database schema, Keys, schema diagrams, Database integrity constraints
: Domain integrity, Entity integrity, Referential integrity, Relational query
languages, Introduction to Relational Algebra, Basic Relational Algebra
Operations, Cartesian Product, Joins, Aggregate functions, Modification of the
databases
3   Introduction to SQL   5
Data Definition Commands, Data Definition Commands, Data Manipulation
Commands, Select Queries, Additional Data Definition Commands, Additional
Select Query Keywords (Book : “Database System Design, Implementation, and
Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
AY 2025-26
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
4   Advanced SQL   3
Set operators, Join operators, Views, Sub-queries (Book : “Database System
Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris,
Peter Bob)
5   Database Design Using the ER Model   5
Overview of the design process, Components of the ER Model, Concepts of ER
model : Mapping Cardinalities, Primary Key, Participation constraints, Weak
entity sets, Generalization, specialization, Converting ER model to Relational
Model, ER model Design issues
6   Relational Database Design   3
Features of Good Relational Designs, Problems with bad design, Decomposition
using concept of functional dependencies, Introduction to process of
normalization and de-normalization. Normal forms – 1NF, 2NF, 3NF, De-
normalization
7   Indexing   2
Introduction to indexes, Need of index in database systems, Types of indexes,
SQL implementation of index
8   Transactions   2
What is transactions? Properties of transaction, Transaction states, Issues with
concurrent executions, Schedules, Serializability –Conflict and View
9   Data Warehousing and Data Mining   3
The need for data analysis, Decision support data, Introduction to Data
Warehouse and Data Mining, Introduction to Online Analytical Processing,
SQL extensions for OLAP- Rollup and Cube Operators (Book : “Database
System Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven
Morris, Peter Bob)
Total   30
Text Books
1. Carlos Coronel and Steven Morris, Database Systems: Design, Implementation and Management, 13th
Edition, Cengage Learning, 2019.
2. Avi Silberschatz, Henry F. Korth, S. Sudarshan, Database System Concepts, 7th Edition, McGraw Hill,
March 2021.
Reference Books
1. Elmasri, Ramez, and Shamakant B. Navathe, Fundamentals of Database Systems, 7th Edition, Pearson
Education Limited, 2017.
2. Gehrke, Johannes and Raghu Ramakrishnan, Database Management Systems, 3rd Edition, McGraw
Hill, 2002.
3.   S. K. Singh, Database Systems Concepts, Design and Applications, 2nd Edition, Pearson Publication, 2009.
4. Coronel, Carlos, Steven Morris, and Peter Rob, Database Systems: Design, Implementation and
Management, 9th Edition, Cengage Learning, 2009.
AY 2025-26
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
_______________________
Signature
(Program Chairperson)
AY 2025-26
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B. Tech Computer Science and
Semester: IV
Engineering (Data Science)- 311 (VT)
````

````text
Course: Database Management Systems Code: 702AI0C001
Teaching Scheme Evaluation Scheme
Lecture Practical Tutorial Internal Continuous Term End

week) week) week) (Marks - 50) (Marks - 100)

Pre-requisite: Nil

Course Objective

The objective of the course is to provide a comprehensive introduction to the fundamental concepts for
design and development of database systems, with an emphasis on how to organize, maintain and retrieve
- efficiently, and effectively - information from a database management system

Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Describe core concepts of database and model a database management system through ER
modelling
2. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structured query language to retrieve and manage
data from relational database
3. Demonstrate the use of normalization for database design
4. Demonstrate the concept of transactions and use modern database techniques such as NoSQL
````

## B TECH · Data Science · AY 2024-25 · Sem 4

- Course as printed: Database Management System (code 702CO0C040)
- Source: `00_Original_Downloads/SYLLABUS/B TECH/11755__2ND YEAR.zip :: COMPUTER ENGINEERING (CSDS) 311/SEM IV/B_Tech_CSEDS_(311)_Sem_IV_AY_2024-25_sMFOxQHHpp.pdf`, page 7-10

````text
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Engineering
Semester: IV
(Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course: Database Management System   Code: 702CO0C040
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per
week)   week)   per week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving, Data structures
Course Objective
To develop an understanding of essential database concepts. To understand different issues
involved in the design and implementation of a database systems. To understand and use database
query languages to access and manage a database. To design and build a simple database system
with modeling, designing and implementation To understand the need of data analytics and
advanced database systems like data warehouse and data mining
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Describe the terminology, features and basic concepts of database systems
2. Formulate queries using structured query language and relational algebra
3. Design a model expressed in the form of an entity relationship diagram and transform it into a
relational model
4. Demonstrate an understanding of normalization theory, transactions and need for data analytics
and advanced database models
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Database Management Systems   3
Role and advantages of the DBMS, Types of Databases, Importance of database
design, Evolution of file system, Problems with file system, Database System
environment, DBMS functions, Data models, importance of data models, types
of data models, Data abstraction (Book   : “Database System Design,
Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
2   Introduction to Relational Model   4
Structure of relational databases- relation, attribute, tuple, domain, Codd’s
rules, Database schema, Keys, schema diagrams, Database integrity constraints
: Domain integrity, Entity integrity, Referential integrity, Relational query
languages, Introduction to Relational Algebra, Basic Relational Algebra
Operations, Cartesian Product, Joins, Aggregate functions, Modification of the
databases
3   Introduction to SQL   5
Data Definition Commands, Data Definition Commands, Data Manipulation
Commands, Select Queries, Additional Data Definition Commands, Additional
Select Query Keywords (Book : “Database System Design, Implementation, and
Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
AY 2024-25
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
4   Advanced SQL   3
Set operators, Join operators, Views, Sub-queries (Book : “Database System
Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris,
Peter Bob)
5   Database Design Using the ER Model   5
Overview of the design process, Components of the ER Model, Concepts of ER
model : Mapping Cardinalities, Primary Key, Participation constraints, Weak
entity sets, Generalization, specialization, Converting ER model to Relational
Model, ER model Design issues
6   Relational Database Design   3
Features of Good Relational Designs, Problems with bad design, Decomposition
using concept of functional dependencies, Introduction to process of
normalization and de-normalization. Normal forms – 1NF, 2NF, 3NF, De-
normalization
7   Indexing   2
Introduction to indexes, Need of index in database systems, Types of indexes,
SQL implementation of index
8   Transactions   2
What is transactions? Properties of transaction, Transaction states, Issues with
concurrent executions, Schedules, Serializability –Conflict and View
9   Data Warehousing and Data Mining   3
The need for data analysis, Decision support data, Introduction to Data
Warehouse and Data Mining, Introduction to Online Analytical Processing,
SQL extensions for OLAP- Rollup and Cube Operators (Book : “Database
System Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven
Morris, Peter Bob)
Total   30
Text Books
1. Carlos Coronel and Steven Morris, Database Systems: Design, Implementation and Management, 13th
Edition, Cengage Learning, 2019.
2. Avi Silberschatz, Henry F. Korth, S. Sudarshan, Database System Concepts, 7th Edition, McGraw Hill,
March 2021.
Reference Books
1. Elmasri, Ramez, and Shamakant B. Navathe, Fundamentals of Database Systems, 7th Edition, Pearson
Education Limited, 2017.
2. Gehrke, Johannes and Raghu Ramakrishnan, Database Management Systems, 3rd Edition, McGraw
Hill, 2002.
3.   S. K. Singh, Database Systems Concepts, Design and Applications, 2nd Edition, Pearson Publication, 2009.
4. Coronel, Carlos, Steven Morris, and Peter Rob, Database Systems: Design, Implementation and
Management, 9th Edition, Cengage Learning, 2009.
AY 2024-25
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
_______________________
Signature
(Program Chairperson)
AY 2024-25
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B. Tech Computer Science and
Semester: IV
Engineering (Data Science)- 311 (VT)
````

## B TECH · Data Science · AY 2023-24 · Sem 6

- Course as printed: Database Management System (code 702CO0C040)
- Source: `00_Original_Downloads/SYLLABUS/B TECH/11961__3RD YEAR.zip :: CSE Data Science (311)/SEM-VI/B_Tech_Computer_Science_and_Engineering_Data_Science__3rd_Year__Sem_VI__A_Y_2023-24_QMmaSqXjFF.pdf`, page 5-8

````text
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Engineering
Semester: VI
(Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course: Database Management System   Code: 702CO0C040
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per
week)   week)   per week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving, Data structures
Course Objective
To develop an understanding of essential database concepts. To understand different issues
involved in the design and implementation of a database systems. To understand and use database
query languages to access and manage a database. To design and build a simple database system
with modeling, designing and implementation To understand the need of data analytics and
advanced database systems like data warehouse and data mining
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Describe the terminology, features and basic concepts of database systems
2. Formulate queries using structured query language and relational algebra
3. Design a model expressed in the form of an entity relationship diagram and transform it into a
relational model
4. Demonstrate an understanding of normalization theory, transactions and need for data analytics
and advanced database models
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Database Management Systems   3
Role and advantages of the DBMS, Types of Databases, Importance of database
design, Evolution of file system, Problems with file system, Database System
environment, DBMS functions, Data models, importance of data models, types
of data models, Data abstraction (Book   : “Database System Design,
Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
2   Introduction to Relational Model   4
Structure of relational databases- relation, attribute, tuple, domain, Codd’s
rules, Database schema, Keys, schema diagrams, Database integrity constraints
: Domain integrity, Entity integrity, Referential integrity, Relational query
languages, Introduction to Relational Algebra, Basic Relational Algebra
Operations, Cartesian Product, Joins, Aggregate functions, Modification of the
databases
3   Introduction to SQL   5
Data Definition Commands, Data Definition Commands, Data Manipulation
Commands, Select Queries, Additional Data Definition Commands, Additional
Select Query Keywords (Book : “Database System Design, Implementation, and
Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
AY 2023-24
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
4   Advanced SQL   3
Set operators, Join operators, Views, Sub-queries (Book : “Database System
Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven Morris,
Peter Bob)
5   Database Design Using the ER Model   5
Overview of the design process, Components of the ER Model, Concepts of ER
model : Mapping Cardinalities, Primary Key, Participation constraints, Weak
entity sets, Generalization, specialization, Converting ER model to Relational
Model, ER model Design issues
6   Relational Database Design   3
Features of Good Relational Designs, Problems with bad design, Decomposition
using concept of functional dependencies, Introduction to process of
normalization and de-normalization. Normal forms – 1NF, 2NF, 3NF, De-
normalization
7   Indexing   2
Introduction to indexes, Need of index in database systems, Types of indexes,
SQL implementation of index
8   Transactions   2
What is transactions? Properties of transaction, Transaction states, Issues with
concurrent executions, Schedules, Serializability –Conflict and View
9   Data Warehousing and Data Mining   3
The need for data analysis, Decision support data, Introduction to Data
Warehouse and Data Mining, Introduction to Online Analytical Processing,
SQL extensions for OLAP- Rollup and Cube Operators (Book : “Database
System Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven
Morris, Peter Bob)
Total   30
Text Books
1. Carlos Coronel and Steven Morris, Database Systems: Design, Implementation and Management, 13th Edition,
Cengage Learning 2019
2. Avi Silberschatz, Henry F. Korth, S. Sudarshan, Database System Concepts, 7th Edition, Mc-Graw Hill,
March 2019.
Reference Books
1. Elmasri, Ramez, and Shamakant B. Navathe, Fundamentals of Database System, 7th Edition, Pearson
Education Limited, 2017
2. Gehrke, Johannes and Raghu Ramakrishnan, Database Management Systems, 3rd Edition, Mc-
Graw Hill, 2014
3. S. K. Singh, Database Systems Concepts, Design and Applications, , 2nd Edition, Pearson
Publication2009
4. Coronel, Carlos, Steven Morris, and Peter Rob, Database Systems: design, implementation and
Management, 9th Edition, Cengage Learning, 2009.
AY 2023-24
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
______________________
Signature
(Program Chairperson)
AY 2023-24
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Engineering (Data
Semester: VI
Science) – 311 (VT)
````

````text
Course: Database Management Systems   Code: 702AI0C001
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
````
````text
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End

week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)

Pre-requisite: Nil
Course Objective
The objective of the course is to provide a comprehensive introduction to the fundamental concepts for
design and development of database systems, with an emphasis on how to organize, maintain and retrieve
- efficiently, and effectively - information from a database management system.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, student will be able to -
1. Describe core concepts of database and model a database management system through ER
modelling
2. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structured query language to retrieve and manage
data from relational database
3. Demonstrate the use of normalization for database design
4. Demonstrate the concept of transactions and use modern database techniques such as NoSQL
````

## B TECH · Data Science · AY 2022-23 · Sem 6

- Course as printed: Database Management System (code 702CO0C040)
- Source: `00_Original_Downloads/SYLLABUS/B TECH/11961__3RD YEAR.zip :: CSE Data Science (311)/SEM-VI/B_Tech_Computer_Science_and_Engineering_Data_Science__3rd_Year__Sem_VI__A_Y_2022-23_tb3WjUQYDr.pdf`, page 5-7

````text
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Engineering
Semester: VI
(Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course: Database Management System   Code: 702CO0C040
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours
Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per   per
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming for Problem Solving, Data structures
Course Objective
To develop an understanding of essential database concepts. To understand different issues
involved in the design and implementation of a database systems. To understand and use
database query languages to access and manage a database. To design and build a simple
database system with modeling, designing and implementation To understand the need of
data analytics and advanced database systems like data warehouse and data mining
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Describe the terminology, features and basic concepts of database systems
2. Formulate queries using structured query language and relational algebra
3. Design a model expressed in the form of an entity relationship diagram and transform it
into a relational model
4. Demonstrate an understanding of normalization theory, transactions and need for data
analytics and advanced database models
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Database Management Systems   3
Role and advantages of the DBMS, Types of Databases, Importance of
database design, Evolution of file system, Problems with file system,
Database System environment, DBMS functions, Data models, importance
of data models, types of data models, Data abstraction (Book : “Database
System Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel,
Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
2   Introduction to Relational Model   4
Structure of relational databases- relation, attribute, tuple, domain, Codd’s
rules, Database schema, Keys, schema diagrams, Database integrity
constraints   : Domain integrity, Entity integrity, Referential integrity,
Relational query languages, Introduction to Relational Algebra, Basic
Relational Algebra Operations, Cartesian Product, Joins, Aggregate
functions, Modification of the databases
3   Introduction to SQL   5
Data   Definition Commands, Data   Definition Commands, Data
Manipulation Commands, Select Queries, Additional Data Definition
Commands, Additional Select Query Keywords (Book : “Database System
Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven
Morris, Peter Bob)
4   Advanced SQL   3
B. Tech CSE(DS) – 311 (VT) / Sem VI / AY 2022-23 / Page 5
SVKM’s NMIMS DEEMED TO BE UNIVERSITY
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Set operators, Join operators, Views, Sub-queries (Book : “Database System
Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel, Steven
Morris, Peter Bob)
5   Database Design Using the ER Model   5
Overview of the design process, Components of the ER Model, Concepts of
ER model : Mapping Cardinalities, Primary Key, Participation constraints,
Weak entity sets, Generalization, specialization, Converting ER model to
Relational Model, ER model Design issues
6   Relational Database Design   3
Features of Good Relational Designs, Problems with bad design,
Decomposition using concept of functional dependencies, Introduction to
process of normalization and de-normalization. Normal forms – 1NF, 2NF,
3NF, De-normalization
7   Indexing   2
Introduction to indexes, Need of index in database systems, Types of
indexes, SQL implementation of index
8   Transactions   2
What is transactions? Properties of transaction, Transaction states, Issues
with concurrent executions, Schedules, Serializability –Conflict and View
9   Data Warehousing and Data Mining   3
The need for data analysis, Decision support data, Introduction to Data
Warehouse and Data Mining, Introduction to Online Analytical Processing,
SQL extensions for OLAP- Rollup and Cube Operators (Book : “Database
System Design, Implementation, and Management”, Carlos Coronel,
Steven Morris, Peter Bob)
Total   30
Text Books
1. Carlos Coronel and Steven Morris, Database Systems: Design, Implementation and Management, 13th
Edition, Cengage Learning 2019
2. Avi Silberschatz, Henry F. Korth, S. Sudarshan, Database System Concepts, 7th Edition, Mc-Graw
Hill, March 2019.
Reference Books
1. Elmasri, Ramez, and Shamakant B. Navathe, Fundamentals of Database System, 7th Edition, Pearson
Education Limited, 2017
2. Gehrke, Johannes and Raghu Ramakrishnan, Database Management Systems, 3rd Edition,
Mc-Graw Hill, 2014
3. S. K. Singh, Database Systems Concepts, Design and Applications, , 2nd Edition, Pearson
Publication2009
4. Coronel, Carlos, Steven Morris, and Peter Rob, Database Systems: design, implementation
and Management, 9th Edition, Cengage Learning, 2009.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
_______________________
Signature
(Program Chairperson)
B. Tech CSE(DS) – 311 (VT) / Sem VI / AY 2022-23 / Page 6
SVKM’s NMIMS DEEMED TO BE UNIVERSITY
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Engineering (Data
Semester: VI
Science) – 311 (VT)
````

- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/MCA/Batch ( 2022 - 2024 )/Semester - 1.pdf`, page 3-5

````text
Program: Master of Computer Application (MCA)   Semester: I
Course: Database Management Systems   Code:
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Internal   Term End
(Hours   Practical   Tutorial   Continuous   Examinations
(Hours   (Hours   Credit
per   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
per week) per week)
week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50 Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Nil
Course Objective
The objective of the course is to provide a comprehensive introduction to the fundamental
concepts for design and development of database systems, with an emphasis on how to
organize, maintain and retrieve - efficiently, and effectively - information from a database
management system.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Describe core concepts of database and model a database management system
through ER modelling
2. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structured query language to retrieve
and manage data from relational database
3. Demonstrate the use of normalization for database design
4. Use modern database techniques such as NoSQL
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction   03
Database System Applications, Purpose of Database Systems, View of
Data, Database Languages, Data Models, Database Users and
Administrator
2   Database Design and the E-R Model   05
Overview of the Design Process, The Entity-Relationship Model,
Constraints, Entity Relationship Diagrams, Reduction to Relational
Schemas, Schema Diagrams   , Entity-Relationship Design Issues,
Extended ER features
3   Introduction to the Relational Model   03
Structure of Relational Databases, Database Schema, Keys, Relational
Algebra, Basic operators of Relational Algebra, Modification of
Databases using Relational Algebra, Database Constraints
4   Structured Query Language   06
Overview of the SQL Query Language, SQL Data Definition, SQL
Constraints, Basic Structure of SQL Queries, Additional Basic
Operations, DML operations, Set operations, Aggregate Functions,
Nested Sub-queries, Joins, views
5   Relational Database Design   05
MCA / Sem I /AY 22-23 Page 3
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Features of Good Relational Designs, Problems with bad design,
Decomposition   using   concept   of   functional   dependencies,
Armstrong’s axioms, Closure of functional dependency, Closure of
attribute, Introduction to process of Normalization and de-
normalization, Normal Forms- 1NF, 2NF, 3NF, BCNF, De-
normalization
6   Transactions   04
What is Transactions? Properties of transaction, Transaction states,
Issues with concurrent executions, Schedules, Serializability- Conflict
and View
7   Introduction to NoSQL   04
Overview of NoSQL, characteristics of NoSQL, Storage types of
NoSQL, Implementing NoSQL in MongoDB - Managing Databases
and Collections from the MongoDB shell, Finding Documents in
MongoDB collection from the MongoDB shell.
Total   30
Text Books:
1. Hennery Korth and Abraham Silberschatz, Database System Concepts, 7th Edition,
McGraw Hill, 2019
2. Gaurav Vaish, Getting Started with NoSQL, 1st edition, Packt Publication, March 2013
3. Brad Daylel, NoSQL with MongoDB in 24 Hours, 1st edition, Sams Teach Yourself,
January 2015
Reference Books:
1. Elmarsi and Navathe, Fundamentals of Database Design, 7th Edition, Addison Wesley,
2019
2. Bob Bryla, Kevin Loney Oracle Database 12C The Complete Reference, 1st edition, Tata
McGraw Hill, 2017
Laboratory/ Tutorial Work
8 to 10 experiments/programming exercises (and a practicum where applicable) based on
the syllabus

(Head of the Department)
````

## B TECH · Computer Science and Business Systems · AY 2021-22 · Sem 4

- Course as printed: Database Management Systems (code 702CO0C017)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH/2ND YEAR/CSBS/SEM IV/B Tech CSBS, 2nd Year, Sem IV, A.Y 2021-22.pdf`, page 4-6

````text
Program: B Tech (Computer Science and Business   Semester: IV
Systems)
Course- Database Management Systems   Module Code: 702CO0C017
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Credit   Internal   Term End
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours   Continuous   Examinations (TEE)
per week)   per   per   Assessment (ICA)   (Marks- 100
week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   in Question Paper)
3   2   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Data Structures, Computer Organization & Architecture
Objectives-
To understand the different issues involved in the design and implementation of a
database system. To model, design and build a simple database system. To understand
and use database query languages to access and manage a database. To understand
query processing and optimization. To develop an understanding of essential DBMS
concepts: Normalization, Transactions, concurrency Control, Storage strategies,
database security and advance topics.
Course Outcomes - After Completion of the Course, students would be able to
1. Understand the terminology, features, types, advantages and characteristics
embodied in database systems,
2. Design database system architecture and data models ,
3. Formulate and solve problems using structure query languages with optimization,
4. Understand indexing techniques, concurrency control techniques, database
security advanced concepts.
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction: Introduction to Database. Hierarchical, Network and   03
Relational Models.
2   Database system architecture: Data Abstraction, Data Independence,   03
Data Definition Language (DDL), Data Manipulation Language
(DML).
3   Data models: Entity-relationship model, network model, relational   06
and object oriented data models, integrity constraints, data
manipulation operations.
4   Relational query languages: Relational algebra, Tuple and domain   06
relational calculus, SQL3, DDL and DML constructs, Open source
and Commercial DBMS - MYSQL, ORACLE, DB2, SQL server.
B. Tech CSBS /Sem IV /A.Y 2021-22 /Page 4
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
5   Relational database design: Domain and data dependency,   05
Armstrong's axioms, Functional Dependencies, Normal forms,
Dependency preservation, Lossless design.
6   Query processing and optimization: Evaluation of relational algebra   05
expressions, Query equivalence, Join strategies, Query optimization
algorithms.
7   Storage strategies: Indices, B-trees, Hashing.   03
8   Transaction processing: Concurrency control, ACID property,   06
Serializability of scheduling, Locking and timestamp based
schedulers, Multi-version and optimistic Concurrency Control
schemes, Database recovery.
9   Database Security: Authentication, Authorization and access control,   04
DAC, MAC and RBAC models, Intrusion detection, SQL injection.
10   Introduction to Advanced topics: Object oriented and object   04
relational databases, Logical databases, Web databases, Distributed
databases, Data warehousing and data mining, Unstructured
database, Dataswarm
Total   45
Text Books:
1. Abraham Silberschatz, Henry F. Korth and S. Sudarshan, “Database System
Concepts”, 6th Edition, McGraw Hill Education, 2013
Reference Books:
1.   J. D. Ullman, “Principles of Database and Knowledge – Base Systems”, 1st Edition,
Computer Science Press, 2016
2. R. Elmasri and S. Navathe, “Fundamentals of Database Systems.”, 7th Edition,
Pearson Education, 2017
3. Abiteboul, Richard Hull, Victor Vianu, “Foundations of Databases”, 1st Edition,
Pearson Publication, 1994
Any other information:

Description of ICA   Marks
Test   20
Term Work   30
````

````text
the MongoDB shell, Finding Documents in MongoDB collection from the

MongoDB shell.

04

Total 30

Text Books
1. Hennery Korth and Abraham Silberschatz, Database System Concepts, 7 Edition, McGraw Hill, 2019.
2. Elmarsi and Navathe, Fundamentals of Database Design, T" Edition, Addison Wesley, 2019.

3. A Phaltankar, J. Ahsan, M. Harrison, L. Nevdov, MondoDB Fundamental, Packt Publishing, 2020.

Reference Books
1. Bob Bryla, Kevin Loney Oracle Database 12C The Complete Reference, 1st Edition, Tata McGraw Hill,
2017.

2. Marko Aleksendric, Arek Borucki, Mastering MongoDB 7.0, Packt Publishing, 2024.

Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
````

## B TECH WORK IN PROCESS · Computer Science and Business Systems · batch 2021-2025 · Sem 4

- Course as printed: Database Management Systems (code 702CO0C017)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH WORK IN PROCESS/Computer Science and Business Systems/Batch 2021 - 2025/Semester 4.pdf`, page 4-6

````text
Program: B Tech (Computer Science and Business   Semester: IV
Systems)
Course- Database Management Systems   Module Code: 702CO0C017
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Credit   Internal   Term End
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours   Continuous   Examinations (TEE)
per week)   per   per   Assessment (ICA)   (Marks- 100
week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   in Question Paper)
3   2   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Data Structures, Computer Organization & Architecture
Objectives-
To understand the different issues involved in the design and implementation of a
database system. To model, design and build a simple database system. To understand
and use database query languages to access and manage a database. To understand
query processing and optimization. To develop an understanding of essential DBMS
concepts: Normalization, Transactions, concurrency Control, Storage strategies,
database security and advance topics.
Course Outcomes - After Completion of the Course, students would be able to
1. Understand the terminology, features, types, advantages and characteristics
embodied in database systems,
2. Design database system architecture and data models ,
3. Formulate and solve problems using structure query languages with optimization,
4. Understand indexing techniques, concurrency control techniques, database
security advanced concepts.
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction: Introduction to Database. Hierarchical, Network and   03
Relational Models.
2   Database system architecture: Data Abstraction, Data Independence,   03
Data Definition Language (DDL), Data Manipulation Language
(DML).
3   Data models: Entity-relationship model, network model, relational   06
and object oriented data models, integrity constraints, data
manipulation operations.
4   Relational query languages: Relational algebra, Tuple and domain   06
relational calculus, SQL3, DDL and DML constructs, Open source
and Commercial DBMS - MYSQL, ORACLE, DB2, SQL server.
B. Tech CSBS /Sem IV /A.Y 2021-22 /Page 4
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
5   Relational database design: Domain and data dependency,   05
Armstrong's axioms, Functional Dependencies, Normal forms,
Dependency preservation, Lossless design.
6   Query processing and optimization: Evaluation of relational algebra   05
expressions, Query equivalence, Join strategies, Query optimization
algorithms.
7   Storage strategies: Indices, B-trees, Hashing.   03
8   Transaction processing: Concurrency control, ACID property,   06
Serializability of scheduling, Locking and timestamp based
schedulers, Multi-version and optimistic Concurrency Control
schemes, Database recovery.
9   Database Security: Authentication, Authorization and access control,   04
DAC, MAC and RBAC models, Intrusion detection, SQL injection.
10   Introduction to Advanced topics: Object oriented and object   04
relational databases, Logical databases, Web databases, Distributed
databases, Data warehousing and data mining, Unstructured
database, Dataswarm
Total   45
Text Books:
1. Abraham Silberschatz, Henry F. Korth and S. Sudarshan, “Database System
Concepts”, 6th Edition, McGraw Hill Education, 2013
Reference Books:
1.   J. D. Ullman, “Principles of Database and Knowledge – Base Systems”, 1st Edition,
Computer Science Press, 2016
2. R. Elmasri and S. Navathe, “Fundamentals of Database Systems.”, 7th Edition,
Pearson Education, 2017
3. Abiteboul, Richard Hull, Victor Vianu, “Foundations of Databases”, 1st Edition,
Pearson Publication, 1994
Any other information:
Total Marks of Internal Continuous Assessment (ICA): 50 Marks
Distribution of ICA Marks:
Description of ICA   Marks
Test   20
Term Work   30
Total Marks :   50
````

````text
What is Transactions? Properties of transaction, Transaction states, Issues
````
````text
with concurrent executions, Schedules, Serializability- Conflict and View
7   Introduction to NoSQL   04
Overview of NoSQL, characteristics of NoSQL, Storage types of NoSQL,
Implementing NoSQL in MongoDB - Managing Databases and Collections
from the MongoDB shell, Finding Documents in MongoDB collection from
the MongoDB shell.
Total   30
Text Books:
1. Hennery Korth and Abraham Silberschatz, Database System Concepts, 7th Edition, McGraw
Hill, 2019
2. Gaurav Vaish, Getting Started with NoSQL, 1st edition, Packt Publication, March 2013
3. Brad Daylel, NoSQL with MongoDB in 24 Hours, 1st edition, Sams Teach Yourself, January 2015
Reference Books:
1. Elmarsi and Navathe, Fundamentals of Database Design, 7th Edition, Addison Wesley, 2019
2. Bob Bryla, Kevin Loney Oracle Database 12C The Complete Reference, 1st edition, Tata McGraw
````

````text
Course: Database Management Systems   Code: 702AI0C001
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Internal   Term End

week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)

Pre-requisite: Nil
Course Objective
The objective of the course is to provide a comprehensive introduction to the fundamental
concepts for design and development of database systems, with an emphasis on how to
organize, maintain and retrieve - efficiently, and effectively - information from a database
management system.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Describe core concepts of database and model a database management system
through ER modelling
2. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structured query language to retrieve
and manage data from relational database
3. Demonstrate the use of normalization for database design
4. Use modern database techniques such as NoSQL
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction   03
Database System Applications, Purpose of Database Systems, View of
Data, Database Languages, Data Models, Database Users and
Administrator
2   Database Design and the E-R Model   05
Overview of the Design Process, The Entity-Relationship Model,
Constraints, Entity Relationship Diagrams, Reduction to Relational
Schemas, Schema Diagrams   , Entity-Relationship Design Issues,
Extended ER features
3   Introduction to the Relational Model   03
Structure of Relational Databases, Database Schema, Keys, Relational
Algebra, Basic operators of Relational Algebra, Modification of
Databases using Relational Algebra, Database Constraints

5   Relational Database Design   05
Features of Good Relational Designs, Problems with bad design,
Decomposition   using   concept   of   functional   dependencies,
````

````text
to

2. Design relational databases,
3. Formulate queries using Relational Algebra and SQL,

and security.

Unit Description   Duration

Systems, Drawbacks of File Systems, Purpose of Database
System, Concept of a Database, Database System Vs File Systems,
04
View of Data, Data Models, Database Languages, Database Users
and Administrator, Transaction Management, Database System
Structures.

Keys, E-R Diagram, Weak Entity Sets, Extended E-R Features, Design
04
Features, Design of Database Schema, Reduction of an E-R schema to

Relational Algebra and Extended Relational-Algebra operations,
Relational Calculus – Tuple Relational Calculus, Domain

Relational Calculus, Formation of Queries, Modification of the

05
Queries, Views, Complex Queries, Database Modification, DDL,

and Authorization in SQL.

Armstrong’s inference rules, Equivalence of sets of FDs, Minimal
sets of FDs, Normalization, Decomposition, First Normal Form to   05
Fifth Normal Form, BCNF, Pitfalls in Relational-Database
Design, Overall database design process.
7   Storage and File Structures: Overview of physical storage media,

organization, Organization of records in files, Data Dictionary
storage.   05
Indexing and Hashing: Basic concepts, Ordered Indices, B+ Tree
Index Files, Static Hashing, Dynamic Hashing, Index Definition
in SQL, Multiple-Key Access.
8   Transactions:   Transaction   concept,   Transaction   state,
Implementation of Atomicity and Durability, Concurrent
04
Executions, Serializability, Recoverability, Implementation of
isolation, Transaction definition in SQL.
9   Concurrency Control: Lock-based protocols, Timestamp-based
protocols, Validation-based protocols, Multiple granularity,
Multiversion schemes, Deadlock handling, Insert and Delete   05
operations, Weak levels of consistency, Concurrency in index
structures.
10   Recovery System: Failure classification, Storage structure,
Recovery and atomicity, Log-based recovery, Shadow paging,   04
Recovery with concurrent transactions, Buffer Management.
````

````text
This course provides understanding of different issues involved in the design and
implementation of a database system and helps students to model, design and build a
simple database system using concepts of ER model and Relational model. It covers use
of database query languages to access and manage a database and to develop an
understanding of essential database management system concepts like Normalization
and Transactions.
Course Outcomes- After completion of the course, students would be able to
1. Derive a model expressed in the form of an entity relation diagram and
transform it into a relational model,
2. Apply normalization theory on a relational model,
3. Formulate solutions to the query problems using structured query language,
4. Demonstrate the concept of transactions.
````

````text
manage data from relational database,
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Computer Programming I and Computer Programming   II, Data
structures.
Objectives: To provide the knowledge of relational database management systems,
design their management and implementation.
Outcomes: After completion of the course, students would be able to:
1. Describe the core concepts of database and model a database management
system through ER modeling.
2. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structural query language to retrieve
and manage data in relational databases.
3. Apply integrity constraints, triggers in database.
4. Describe Normalization, indexing, transaction management, concurrency and
recovery in database management system.
Detailed Syllabus: ( per session plan )
Unit Description   Duration
1. Introductory Database concepts: Introduction to Data Processing,   04
Overview of Files Systems, Drawbacks of File Systems, Purpose of
Database System, Concept of a Database, Comparison of Database
System and File Systems, View of Data, Data Models, Database
Languages, Database   Users and   Administrator,   Transaction
Management, Database System Structures and architecture.
2. Entity Relationship Model: Components of ER Model- Entity,   05
Attributes and Relationships, Sample ER Model (Case Discussion),
Constraints,   Converting Extended ER features, Issues of Entity
Relationship Model.
3. Relational Model: Basic Concept of RDBMS- Relation, Attribute,   06
Tuple and Domain, Codd’s Rules, Concept of keys in RDBMS,
Database Integrity Constraints- Domain Integrity, Entity Integrity &
Referential Integrity, Database Schema Diagram. The relational
algebra and extended relational algebra operations, Formation of

Aggregate Functions, Null Values, Nested Queries, Views, Complex
Queries. Stored Procedures and Functions.
5. Integrity and Security: Domain Constrains, Referential Integrity,   02
Assertions, Triggers, Triggers and Assertions in SQL, Security and
Authorization in SQL.
6. Relational Database Design: Goals of Normalization, Functional   06
Dependencies (FD) – FD closures & Key determination process,
Decomposition: Properties of decomposition, Normal Forms: 1NF,
2NF, 3NF and BCNF, Sample problems to convert Non 1NF table into
Normalized Tables.
7. Indexing and Hashing: Basic concepts, Ordered Indices – dense,   04
Sparse Index, Multilevel Indices, Static Hashing, Dynamic Hashing,
Comparison of Indexing and Hashing, Indexing in SQL.
8. Transactions: Basic Concept of Transaction, State of a Transaction,   04
Implementation of Atomicity and Durability, Concurrent Executions,
Serializability,   Testing   for   Serializability,   Recoverability,
Implementation of Isolation, Transaction in SQL.
9. Concurrency Control and Recovery: Lock based protocols, Timestamp   06
based protocols, validation based protocols, Deadlock handling,

based recovery and shadow paging.

system and banking system.
````

````text
4, Use database for concurrent use and Backup data from database.

Unit | Description
1. | Introductory concepts of DBMS:

Introduction and applications of DBMS, Purpose of data base, 03
Data, Independence, Database System architecture- levels,
Mappings, Database, users and DBA

2, Relational Model:
Structure of relational databases, Domains, Relations, Relational 04
algebra - fundamental operators and syntax, relational algebra
queries, tuple relational calculus

3. Entity-Relationship model:
Basic concepts, Design process, constraints, Keys, Design issues, 04
E-R diagrams, weak entity sets, extended ER features -
generalization, specialization, aggregation, reduction to E-R
database schema.

Duration

27

Electronics & Telecommunication (2019 - 2020)

4. Relational Database design:
Functional Dependency - definition, trivial and non-trivial FD, 05
closure of FD set, closure of attributes, irreducible set of FD,
Normalization - 1Nf, 2NF, 3NF, Decomposition using FD-
dependency preservation, BCNF, Multivalued dependency, 4NF,
Join dependency and 5NF.

5. Query Processing & Query Optimization:
Overview, measures of query cost, selection operation, sorting,
join, evaluation of expressions, transformation of relational 05
expressions, estimating statistics of expression results, evaluation
plans, materialized views

6. Transaction Management: 09
Transaction concepts, properties of transactions, serializability of
transactions, testing for serializability, System recovery, Two-

Phase Commit protocol, Recovery and Atomicity, Log-based
recovery, concurrent executions of transactions and related
problems, Locking mechanism, solution to concurrency related
problems, deadlock, , two-phase locking protocol, Isolation,

Intent locking

Introduction, Discretionary access control, Mandatory Access
Control.

8. SQL Concepts: 10

Basics of SQL, DDL,DML,DCL, structure - creation, alteration,
defining constraints - Primary key, foreign key, unique, not null,
check, IN operator, Functions - aggregate functions, Built-in
functions - numeric, date, string functions, set operations, sub-
queries, correlated sub-queries, Use of group by, having, order
by, join and its types, Exist, Any, All , view and its types.
transaction control commands - Commit, Roilback, Savepoint

9. PL/SQL Concepts: 03
Cursors, Stored Procedures, Stored Function, Database Triggers.

Total Hours 45
````

````text
algebra - fundamental operators and syntax, relational algebra

expressions, estimating statistics of expression results, evaluation
plans, materialized views

locking

check, IN operator, Functions - aggregate functions, Built-in
functions - numeric, date, string functions, set operations, sub-
queries, correlated sub-queries, Use of group by, having, order by,
join and its types, Exist, Any, All , view and its types. transaction
control commands - Commit, Rollback, Savepoint

Cursors, Stored Procedures, Stored Function, Database Triggers.
````

````text
design their management and implementation.

Course Outcomes: After successful completion of this course, students will be able to

1. Describe the core concepts of database and model a database management
system through ER modeling.

2. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structural query language to retrieve
and manage data in relational databases.

3. Apply integrity constraints and triggers in database.

4. Illustrate the concept of Normalization.

5. Discover the importance of indexing, transaction management, concurrency
and recovery in database management system.
````

````text
Objectives:

To provide the knowledge of relational database management systems, design their

management and implementation.

Course Outcomes: After successful completion of this course, students will be able to

1. Describe the core concepts of database.

2. Design a relational database management through Entity Relationship
modeling.

3. Apply knowledge of relational algebra and structural query language to
retrieve and manage data in relational databases.

4, Discover the importance of concurrency, security, storage, recovery, indexing
and transaction management in database management system.

Detailed Syllabus:
Unit | Description Duration
1. Introductory Database concepts: Introduction to Data 04

Processing, Overview of Files Systems, Drawbacks of File Systems,
Purpose of Database System, Concept of a Database, Database
System Vs File Systems, View of Data, Data Models, Database
Languages, Database Users and Administrator, Transaction
Management, Database System Structures.

Reduction of an E-R schema to Tables

Relational Model: Concept of relation, Notion of primary and 04
secondary keys, foreign keys, Structure of relational databases,

The relational algebra and extended relational algebra operations,
Formation of queries, Modification of the databases, views.

Structured Query Language (SQL): Background, Basic Structure, 04
Set Operations, Aggregate Functions, Null Values, Nested
Queries, Views, Complex Queries, Database Modification, DDL,

Features.

and Authorization in SQL.

Relational Database Design: Features of good relational 07
database design, Atomic domains and _ first normal form,
Decomposition using functional dependencies, Functional
dependency theory, Normalization, Decomposition, First Normal

Form to Fifth Normal Form, BCNEF, Pitfalls in Relational-
Database Design.

Storage and File Structure: Overview of physical storage media, 04
Magnetic disks, RAID, Tertiary storage, Storage Access, File
organization, Organization of records in Files, Data Dictionary
storage.

Indexing and Hashing: Basic concepts, Ordered Indices - dense, 03
Sparse Index, Multilevel Indices, Static Hashing, Dynamic
Hashing, Comparison of Indexing and Hashing, Indexing in SQL.

Transactions: Basic Concept of Transaction, State of a 03
Transaction, Implementation of Atomicity and Durability,

10. | Concurrency Control: Lock based protocols, Timestamp based 05
````

````text
concurrency, security and transaction management.

them to build query in SQL.
````

````text
Unit | Description Duration

1 | Introduction: Introduction to Data Processing, Overview of Files
Systems, Drawbacks of File Systems, Purpose of Database
System, Concept of a Database, Database System Vs File Systems,

4
View of Data, Data Models, Database Languages, Database Users
and Administrator, Transaction Management, Database System
Structures.

Keys, E-R Diagram, Weak Entity Sets, Extended E-R Features, Design 4

Features, Design of Database Schema, Reduction of an E-R schema to

5 | Integrity and Security: Domain Constrains, Referential Integrity,

and Authorization in SQL.

6 | Relational-Database Design: Functional § dependency,
Armstrong's inference rules, Equivalence of sets of FDs, Minimal
sets of FDs, Normalization, Decomposition, First Normal Form to
Fifth Normal Form, BCNF, Pitfalls in Relational-Database Design,
Overall database design process.

7 | Storage and File Structures: Overview of physical storage media,

organization, Organization of records in files, Data Dictionary

storage.

Index Files, Static Hashing, Dynamic Hashing, Index Definition

8 | Transactions: Transaction concept, Transaction _ state,

Executions, Serializability, Recoverability, Implementation of
isolation, Transaction definition in SQL.

9 | Concurrency Control: Lock-based protocols, Timestamp-based
protocols, Validation-based protocols, Multiple granularity,

operations, Weak levels of consistency, Concurrency in index
structures.
````

````text
indexing and transaction management in database management system.
````
